# Phase 3 — Provider Source Register

**Status: PLANNING. NOTHING HAS BEEN PURCHASED, TRIALLED OR CREDENTIALED.**

Every provider claim made anywhere in `docs/phase3/` carries a **claim id** that resolves
to a row in this register. Each row names the exact source a reviewer can open, the exact
claim that source supports, and the limitation on that claim.

> **Evidence grades are not citations.** Revision 1 of the plan graded its claims but did
> not make them retrievable, so a reviewer could not check any of them. This register is the
> correction. **No claim is marked `[V]` without an official source a reviewer can open.**

| | |
|---|---|
| research performed | **2026-08-26** (round 1) · **2026-08-27** (round 2, the `R2` section below) |
| claim records | **542** |
| by grade | `V` 379 · `V2` 79 · `Q` 16 · `U` 68 |
| by source type | `OFFICIAL` 392 · `SECONDARY` 79 · `UNAVAILABLE` 71 |

Round 2 is the maintenance rule at the end of this document exercised for the G1/G3 decision
packet. It re-verified the licensing and pricing rows that a purchase would rest on, read four
Sharadar licence sections that round 1 recorded as unread, and answered several round-1 `U`
rows. **It bought nothing, credentialed nothing and retrieved no vendor data.**

## How to read a row

| Grade | Source type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **`V`** | `OFFICIAL` | The vendor's own page was retrieved on the accessed date and states the claim. A reviewer can open the URL and see it. |
| **`V2`** | `SECONDARY` | A credible non-vendor source states it — a reseller, a library guide, a search-index summary, a press release. Weaker. Re-verify before relying on it. |
| **`Q`** | `OFFICIAL` / `UNAVAILABLE` | No public price exists; a sales quote is required. |
| **`U`** | `UNAVAILABLE` | Could not be retrieved — gated, 403, JavaScript-only, or simply not stated anywhere. The `qualification` column records what was tried. |

**A `U` row is evidence too.** "No vendor page states the historical depth" is a finding, and
it is the reason two of this plan's candidate providers remain candidates rather than choices.

## Licensing note on this register itself

This register quotes **public documentation, published prices and licence terms**. It
contains **no** empirical evaluation of any vendor's data, because no vendor's data has been
obtained. That distinction is deliberate: at least one candidate's terms forbid publishing
conclusions about the usability or fitness of their data without permission
(`PSR-SHD` licensing rows). Planning comparisons drawn from public documents are not covered
by that clause; empirical data-quality reports would be, which is why
[data-quality-plan.md](data-quality-plan.md) keeps those under `.runtime/` and uncommitted.

Starting from $4,800' consulting block, none to a tier price); raw curl of /upgrade and /pricing/researcher (both ~400 KB, ZERO dollar figures in static HTML). Prices are rendered only after JS executes and an organization context loads. Third-party reviews quote conflicting figures ($10/mo vs $60/mo) and were not used. This gap affects total cost of ownership, since CLI/local access requires a paid tier. |

What I tried: https://sharadar.com/docs/fundamentals fetched three times, including one pass whose prompt explicitly asked about amended filings (10-K/A, 10-Q/A), multiple rows per ticker+dimension+reportperiod, and whether AR stores every successive version — the fetch returned that the page "does not directly address amended filings, revision history, or whether AR dimensions store successive versions"; https://sharadar.com/docs/faqs (restatement FAQ covers only AR-vs-MR); https://sharadar.com/docs/descriptions (indicator definitions not exposed); the Quandl/Nasdaq Sharadar datasheet PDF (says only "Data including or excluding restatements"). Suggestive but NOT probative counter-evidence exists in both directions: the AR primary key is (ticker, dimension, date, reportperiod), which would permit several filing dates for one reporting period, but the docs never say it does. Do not claim a full revision chronology on this evidence. Verifying it requires querying the API for a known restater and counting rows per reportperiod — an empirical test, not a documentation claim. |
| `PSR-SHD-080` | Sharadar | `UNAVAILABLE` | **`U`** | The exact annual price of the Fundamentals, Prices, Investors and Bundle plans could not be established. | [Annual (yearly) price for any Sharadar Direct plan](https://sharadar.com/subscribe) | 2026-08-26 | Tried: two WebFetch passes on https://sharadar.com/subscribe with prompts explicitly asking for annual/yearly pricing and toggles; one WebFetch on https://sharadar.com/ ; one on https://sharadar.com/docs/faqs. Annual billing is confirmed to exist but no figure is published on any publicly reachable page found. Obtaining it appears to require signing in to the checkout flow, which was out of scope. Any annual-cost estimate is therefore unverified. |

## NRG — Norgate Data

| claim_id | provider | type | grade | claim | source | accessed | qualification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PSR-NRG-001` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "We specialize in survivorship bias-free data for US, Australian and Canadian stock markets." | [Norgate Data — homepage (survivorship bias)](https://norgatedata.com/) | 2026-08-26 | Vendor marketing claim on their own homepage. Materially qualified elsewhere on the same site: survivorship-bias-free behaviour requires Platinum or Diamond (delisted securities are absent from Silver/Gold), and the vendor disclaims completeness of the delisted set for the 1950s–early 1960s. Treat as a positioning statement whose operative truth is tier-dependent. |
| `PSR-NRG-002` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Direct access to the database is provided by way of plugins into supported 3rd-party charting/analysis applications" and "Data updates are performed by our Norgate Data Updater application." | [Norgate Data — homepage (delivery mechanism)](https://norgatedata.com/) | 2026-08-26 | ARCHITECTURALLY SIGNIFICANT AND EASILY MISSED: Norgate is NOT a cloud REST API. It is a Windows desktop updater (Norgate Data Updater) maintaining a LOCAL database, read through plugins (AmiBroker, RightEdge, Wealth-Lab) or the Python/Zipline packages against that local store. Implies a Windows host and a scheduled local update step in any production pipeline, and no server-side query endpoint. The homepage does not state OS requirements explicitly — a separate System Requirements page exists that was not retrieved. |
| `PSR-NRG-003` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "A security is only included in Delisted if it traded on a major exchange at some point in its history and is no longer tradeable. A security that has been relegated from a major exchange, but can still be traded on OTC, is not considered to be delisted." | [Norgate Data — Data Content Tables (delisted definition)](https://norgatedata.com/data-content-tables.php) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved as raw HTML via curl (HTTP 200, 181,901 bytes) and text-extracted, so the wording is the page's own. This is a materially strict definition: a stock that falls to OTC stays in the LISTED universe until it stops trading entirely, which affects how a backtest universe filter must be written. Does not state how the major-exchange test is applied historically. |
| `PSR-NRG-004` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "The symbol for a delisted security has a year & month suffix indicating when the security last traded - e.g. ALOG-201806." | [Norgate Data — Data Content Tables (delisted symbol convention)](https://norgatedata.com/data-content-tables.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. Operationally important: delisted tickers are NOT bare symbols, so any symbol-matching or broker-mapping code must handle the -YYYYMM suffix. Applies to US; the page gives the parallel conventions for AU (e.g. ONE-200105.au) and CA (e.g. A-200710.ca). |
| `PSR-NRG-005` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "There is no claim that the database for Delisted is \"complete\", especially with regard to the 1950s and early 1960s. It is, however, extensive, containing 25222 delisted securities from the start of 1950 to Sep 2022, and will continue to increase as additional securities are delisted." | [Norgate Data — Data Content Tables (delisted count and completeness disclaimer)](https://norgatedata.com/data-content-tables.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. THIS FIGURE IS STALE ON ITS FACE: the count is stated as of Sep 2022, roughly four years before the 2026-08-26 access date, and the page itself says the count keeps growing, so 25,222 is a floor, not a current number. The explicit non-completeness disclaimer for the 1950s–early 1960s is a direct limit on how far back a survivorship-bias-free backtest can honestly claim to be clean. |
| `PSR-NRG-006` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Capital reconstructions include splits & consolidations (reverse splits), bonus issues (stock dividends) and rights issues. Special distributions include capital returns, special dividends and distributions associated with spin-offs." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (price adjustment events)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved as raw HTML via curl (HTTP 200, 45,334 bytes). Note the URL is data-package-faq.php — the guessed paths data-faq.php, datafaq.php and datacontents.php all returned HTTP 404. Enumerates which corporate-action classes are handled; does NOT state data sourcing, vendor for the corporate-action feed, or error rates. |
| `PSR-NRG-007` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | For a 2-for-1 split "all historical prices before the ex-date of the split are multiplied by 0.5 ... At the same time, historical volumes are multiplied by 2"; for distributions, "Adjustment Factor = $90/$100 = 0.9" and "All prices before the ex-date of the distribution are multiplied by 0.9". | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (adjustment factor method)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified; the text sits inside a JS show/hide block (style="display:none") so it is present in source but NOT visible on the rendered page until the reader clicks — a browser-only reviewer may not see it without expanding. Confirms standard back-adjustment (multiplicative, applied to pre-ex-date bars) and that volume is adjusted inversely. Does not specify float/rounding precision or how adjustment interacts with the unadjusted series. |
| `PSR-NRG-008` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Asked "Is the database static with regard to historical data?" the answer is "No." — corrections apply to "Trade corrections/cancellations, Index prices and volumes, Listing status ..., Dividend type, Dividend ex-date, Sector/Industry classification, Surviving entity determination (mergers & acquisitions)" and "There is no notification of such corrections - they are applied to your database on a continuous basis whilst your subscription is active." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (database is not static)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. MATERIAL FOR A POINT-IN-TIME DATA PLAN: Norgate silently restates history with no correction notifications, so the local database is NOT immutable and a backtest is not automatically reproducible across update cycles. Norgate is survivorship-bias-free but this shows it is not a full point-in-time/vintage archive — it does not retain as-was snapshots. Does not state correction frequency or magnitude. |
| `PSR-NRG-009` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Asked "Do you sell, or provide, lists of historical index constituents?" the answer is "No. However, we provide useful access to index constituents via supported environments." and "There are no lists showing historical additions to, or removals from, an index. Nor are there any lists showing constituents at year-end". | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (no constituent lists sold)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. Important scoping limit: historical index membership is queryable only as a per-symbol, per-date boolean through the plugin/API (see the index_constituent_timeseries record), NOT as a downloadable membership table or an add/drop event log. Anyone needing a reconstitution event history must derive it themselves. Does not say whether deriving and storing such a list is permitted under licence. |
| `PSR-NRG-010` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Temporary inclusions (aka temporary lines) are not included in historical index constituents. These are typically events, such as demergers, where a demerged company is temporarily an index constituent for a limited period (such as one day) and is then removed." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (temporary inclusions excluded)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. A known, disclosed gap in point-in-time index membership — short-lived index members are absent. Low impact for a 2–30 day swing horizon but it is a real deviation from true point-in-time index reconstruction. Does not quantify how many such events exist. |
| `PSR-NRG-011` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "we provide access to a field called assetid. This field does not change over the lifetime of a security, including through symbol changes, company name changes, moves between exchanges, transitions to or from OTC trading, delisting, or other corporate actions." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (stable asset identifier)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. Provides a stable primary key for a local security master, which is the correct join key rather than ticker. Qualified by the adjacent FAQ answer that "only the current symbol is provided" — historical ticker strings are NOT supplied, so ticker-based reconciliation against external/broker records for past dates is not directly supported. Availability stated as "in supported software packages and environments", so not guaranteed in every integration. |
| `PSR-NRG-012` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Norgate provides only the current symbol for a security, not historical symbols: "No, only the current symbol is provided. Commentary: Since the historical price data is prepended to the new symbol for each security, this is typically not needed." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (historical symbols not provided)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. Direct consequence: point-in-time ticker reconstruction is impossible from Norgate alone, which matters for matching against news, filings or broker fills keyed on the as-of ticker. Norgate argues it is "typically not needed" for backtesting; that argument holds for price-series continuity, not for cross-source joins on ticker. |
| `PSR-NRG-013` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Index volume and turnover are calculated by Norgate based upon the constituents in the index. The calculation uses point-in-time constituents." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (index volume uses point-in-time constituents)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. One of the few explicit uses of the phrase "point-in-time" by the vendor, and it is scoped narrowly to index volume/turnover computation — it is NOT a general claim that the whole database is point-in-time, and it does not contradict the "database is not static" answer recorded separately. |
| `PSR-NRG-014` | Norgate Data | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Historical coverage of all US-listed securities becomes less complete further back in time, mostly due to limitations in the trade-reporting requirements imposed at the time." | [Norgate Data — Data Package FAQ (coverage degrades with age)](https://norgatedata.com/data-package-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Raw-HTML verified. Directly tempers the Diamond tier's "back to 1950" headline: depth of start date is not the same as breadth of cross-section. No quantification of how incomplete, or from which year degradation becomes material, is given. |

Arithmetic check against stockmarketpackages.php is consistent: Silver 148.50 x 2 = 297, and 270 is 9.09% below that (i.e. 270 = 297 x 0.909), so "10% discount" is approximate/marketing rounding rather than exact. Region-neutral statement. |

Establishes that UNADJUSTED prices are available (NONE) alongside three adjustment modes. This is the concrete mechanism behind the FAQ's refusal to sell constituent lists — membership is a per-symbol boolean time series. Does not establish which indices are supported, the date range of membership data, or the licence terms governing storing derived membership tables. Requires a Platinum/Diamond subscription plus the local Norgate Data Updater to return anything. |

Prices are computed client-side after user selections, so they are not reachable without executing JS and making selections. Does NOT mean Norgate hides prices — the same prices ARE published statically on stockmarketpackages.php (see separate records). Only the calculator page is U. |

## DBN — Databento

| claim_id | provider | type | grade | claim | source | accessed | qualification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PSR-DBN-001` | Databento | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | DBEQ.BASIC is "a bundle of US equities proprietary feeds from NYSE Chicago, NYSE National, IEX, and MIAX Pearl Equities, which are free to license for distribution, display, and non-display applications", with "No monthly licensing fees from the exchange". | [Databento Blog — Introducing Databento Equities Basic (DBEQ.BASIC)](https://databento.com/blog/dbeq-basic) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved directly from the vendor blog. IMPORTANT COVERAGE LIMIT: these four venues are LOW-MARKET-SHARE feeds — the bundle is licence-free precisely because it excludes the major-volume venues (Nasdaq, NYSE primary, Cboe). It is therefore not a consolidated tape and not a substitute for full-market EOD prices. The blog post does not state the bundle's percentage of US equities volume. |
| `PSR-DBN-002` | Databento | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | DBEQ.BASIC historical data is available from April 2023, with usage-based pricing or a flat rate of "$825 per month" for unlimited historical and real-time access. | [Databento Blog — Introducing Databento Equities Basic (start date and flat rate)](https://databento.com/blog/dbeq-basic) | 2026-08-26 | From the vendor blog. The $825/month figure predates the January 2025 pricing restructure (Standard $199 / Plus $1,750 / Unlimited $4,500) announced by Databento itself, so it may be SUPERSEDED — the blog post is undated in my retrieval and I could not confirm it against the current pricing page, which does not list an $825 tier. Treat the price as historical unless re-verified. The April 2023 start date is the durable fact. |
| `PSR-DBN-003` | Databento | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Databento users don't need a license to access historical data, which we define as 24 hours into the past." | [Databento Blog — Part 1: Introduction to market data licensing](https://databento.com/blog/introduction-market-data-licensing) | 2026-08-26 | THIS IS THE 24-HOUR STATEMENT REQUESTED. Source is Databento's own blog, not a contract — it is an explanatory article, NOT the binding licence agreement or terms of service, and the actual executed agreement governs. The definition is Databento's own ("which we define as"); the same article notes venues differ, with some defining historical as 24 hours back and others as right after the session ends. |
| `PSR-DBN-004` | Databento | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "You need a license to access real-time (live, intraday) market data if you're a professional user or if you're distributing data externally within 24 hours of receipt. You do NOT need a license to access historical (T+1) data." | [Databento Blog — Part 1: Introduction to market data licensing (redistribution)](https://databento.com/blog/introduction-market-data-licensing) | 2026-08-26 | THE REDISTRIBUTION QUOTE REQUESTED, verbatim. Note the trigger is EXTERNAL distribution within 24 hours of receipt — internal use by a single trading system is not distribution. Blog article, not the licence agreement; binding terms are in the executed agreement and Databento's ToS, which I did not retrieve (they were not among the given URLs and some sit behind the JS SPA). A separate search result indicated redistribution of even historical data can require a licence, which is in tension with the flat 'do NOT need a license' phrasing — resolve against the actual contract, not this article. |

Establishes that pay-as-you-go survives for HISTORICAL data but was removed for LIVE data — relevant only if live feeds are ever in scope. Post is forward-looking from early 2025 and is now ~19 months old as of 2026-08-26, so treat the plan structure as confirmed by the current pricing page rather than by this post. Does not state per-GB rates or minimum spend. |
| `PSR-DBN-006` | Databento | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Databento US Equities historical data is advertised "from $0.40/GB". | [Databento — Equities (headline per-GB rate)](https://databento.com/equities) | 2026-08-26 | TREAT WITH CAUTION — ATTRIBUTION WAS UNSTABLE ACROSS READS. A first WebFetch of this page rendered a table attributing $0.40/GB to Nasdaq TotalView-ITCH (XNAS.ITCH) and repeating it for other venues; a second, deliberately stricter WebFetch of the SAME URL attributed "from $0.40/GB" to the Databento US Equities proprietary product and returned "not shown" for every venue dataset. I am reporting the stricter read. The first read's per-dataset table should be considered an extraction artifact and not relied upon. "from" signals a floor price, not the rate for any specific schema or dataset. Verify in the calculator before using in a budget. |

Verified on the vendor's /equities page under a strict prompt, and closely corroborated by the vendor's 'Introducing Databento US Equities' blog which words it as "across all NMS exchanges and ATSs ... without introducing license fees". Crucially this page does NOT state the dataset's history start date — see the separate record on why depth, not existence, is the disqualifier. "Delayed basis" means it is not a real-time product. |

"16+ years" is a floor phrased by the vendor and is not per-dataset; individual US equities datasets have much later start dates (XNAS.ITCH ~2018, proprietary EQUS/DBEQ ~2023) — see separate records. Does not define L0/L1/L2/L3 on this page. |

Makes iterative/exploratory research over tick data expensive in a way a naive $/GB reading understates. Page does not give the rate itself. |

NOT acted upon: per the task's prohibitions I did not sign up, register, or create an account to obtain or verify these credits. Credit applies to historical data only, not live. Terms may have conditions not shown on the pricing page. |
| `PSR-DBN-013` | Databento | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Databento's published plan structure caps deep history at L0 on the $199 Standard plan (1 year L1, 1 month L2/L3), billing is per uncompressed GB of tick-granular data, and its US equities datasets begin in the late 2010s to 2023 — so it functions as a tick/L1-L3 microstructure provider rather than a deep-history daily-bar vendor. | [Databento — suitability of Databento for multi-decade daily-bar US equity history](https://databento.com/pricing) | 2026-08-26 | THIS IS AN ANALYTIC CONCLUSION I DREW, not a sentence any vendor page states — graded V only because each constituent fact is separately V/V2 above (plan history tiers from the pricing page; per-uncompressed-GB billing from the pricing page; EQUS.MINI ~March 2023 and DBEQ.BASIC April 2023 start dates; XNAS.ITCH ~2018 at V2/weak). The nuance that must not be lost: EQUS.SUMMARY IS a genuine consolidated end-of-day OHLCV product, so 'they don't do EOD' would be wrong — the disqualifier is HISTORY DEPTH (~3 years) and the total absence of any delisted-security or historical-index-constituent product, not the absence of daily bars. Anyone needing survivorship-bias-free multi-decade daily history cannot get it from Databento at any tier. Re-verify the start dates before treating this as final. |

Do not rely on 2018 without re-verification; the directional point — that even Databento's deepest US equities venue history starts in the late 2010s, not the 1990s — is what matters and is consistent across sources. |

NautilusTrader also flags a subtle semantic: Databento timestamps bars at the START of the aggregation interval (ts_event = bar open time) and Nautilus normalizes this to the bar close — an off-by-one-interval hazard for anyone consuming daily bars directly. What IS established is that Plus and Unlimited require an annual contract, which is a commitment of a different kind. Confirm directly with Databento before assuming zero floor. |

## EST — Analyst estimates and revisions

| claim_id | provider | type | grade | claim | source | accessed | qualification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PSR-EST-001` | Bloomberg | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Bloomberg CoFi "provides corporate action adjusted point-in-time historical and ongoing company actuals, consensus estimates, company guidance, pricing and security master data in one connected solution." | [Company Financials, Estimates and Pricing Point-in-Time (CoFi) \| Bloomberg Professional Services](https://professional.bloomberg.com/products/data/enterprise-catalog/cofi/) | 2026-08-26 | Confirms a second genuine PIT consensus product exists. Note "corporate action adjusted" — the opposite convention to FactSet PIT, which explicitly does NOT adjust for dilutions; the two products therefore are not drop-in substitutes and a factor built on one will not reproduce exactly on the other. The page does not define the snapshot cut-off time or cadence. |
| `PSR-EST-002` | Bloomberg | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Bloomberg CoFi states "17 years of history" across "85k Companies" with "530+ Data fields", and updates data for 5,000+ major index companies "the same day their earnings are released", with other global companies processed "within 24 hours of filing". | [Company Financials, Estimates and Pricing Point-in-Time (CoFi) \| Bloomberg Professional Services](https://professional.bloomberg.com/products/data/enterprise-catalog/cofi/) | 2026-08-26 | 17 years from 2026 implies roughly a 2009 start — comparable to FactSet PIT and likewise excluding the 2008 crisis. The 85k company and 530+ field figures span the whole CoFi product (financials + estimates + pricing + security master), not the estimates component alone. Update timeliness quoted is for actuals/filings, not for estimate revisions. |
| `PSR-EST-003` | FactSet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "The Point-in-Time database provides daily consensus data relative to the local midnight for each company. The consensus snapshot ensures the data that has been used for the calculation was available at the date of this calculation." | [At a Glance: FactSet Estimates Point-in-Time Consensus](https://insight.factset.com/resources/at-a-glance-factset-estimates-point-in-time-consensus) | 2026-08-26 | This is the clearest genuine PIT-consensus specification obtained from any vendor: a DAILY, per-company, local-midnight snapshot. Caveats: it is a daily snapshot, so intraday as-of reconstruction is not supported; "local midnight" means the effective cut-off varies by listing venue and must be handled explicitly; and the source is FactSet's Insight resource site rather than the product catalogue page (which was unretrievable — see the UNAVAILABLE record). |
| `PSR-EST-004` | FactSet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | FactSet Estimates Point-in-Time Consensus is "comprised of over 800 contributors, covering the markets of 90 countries" with "10+ years of history", records dating back to December 2009, daily updates and "Intraday updates three times per day". | [At a Glance: FactSet Estimates Point-in-Time Consensus](https://insight.factset.com/resources/at-a-glance-factset-estimates-point-in-time-consensus) | 2026-08-26 | HISTORY DEPTH IS THE BINDING LIMITATION: December 2009 onwards is roughly 16 years as of 2026 and excludes the 2008 crisis entirely. Note the internal tension between "daily consensus data relative to the local midnight" (the PIT snapshot) and "intraday updates three times per day" (the delivery cadence of the current file) — these are different things and the page does not fully disambiguate them. |
| `PSR-EST-005` | FactSet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "The Point-in-Time database is not adjusted for dilutions, QA corrections, changes to the default currency, or broker estimates that were not available as of the consensus date." | [At a Glance: FactSet Estimates Point-in-Time Consensus](https://insight.factset.com/resources/at-a-glance-factset-estimates-point-in-time-consensus) | 2026-08-26 | This is the correct PIT behaviour (no retroactive restatement) but it shifts real work onto the consumer: split/dilution adjustment and currency normalisation must be applied by the user, and known data errors are deliberately NOT corrected in history. Any surprise calculation joining PIT estimates to actuals must handle share-count changes itself. |
| `PSR-EST-006` | FactSet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The FactSet Consensus Estimates DataFeed comprises "Consensus Estimates: Current and historical snapshots of broker consensus estimates" and "Detail Estimates: Current and historical estimate values tied to individual analysts and brokers", plus broker and company-reported actuals and guidance values. | [At a Glance: FactSet Consensus Estimates DataFeed](https://insight.factset.com/resources/factset-consensus-estimates-datafeed) | 2026-08-26 | Confirms BOTH consensus-level historical snapshots and analyst/broker-level detail exist in the same feed family. It does not state the snapshot cadence for this feed (the separate Point-in-Time Consensus product is where the daily-midnight guarantee is documented), nor whether "historical snapshots" here carry the same no-restatement guarantee. Treat the DataFeed and the PIT Consensus product as distinct SKUs. |
| `PSR-EST-007` | FactSet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Historical estimates are available globally starting in 1999 with coverage for European companies extending back to 1997", covering "over 16,000 active companies across 90 countries" from over 800 contributors, with intraday updates and "20+ years of history". | [At a Glance: FactSet Consensus Estimates DataFeed](https://insight.factset.com/resources/factset-consensus-estimates-datafeed) | 2026-08-26 | Note the divergence from the PIT Consensus product (December 2009): the DataFeed's history reaches back to 1999 globally, but the daily point-in-time snapshot guarantee is documented only for the PIT product from 2009. Pre-2009 DataFeed history should NOT be assumed to be point-in-time. "16,000 active companies" also differs from the 19,000+ figure that appears in FactSet marketing summaries elsewhere. |
| `PSR-EST-008` | Intrinio | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Intrinio serves OHLCV price bars via https://api-v2.intrinio.com/securities/{identifier}/prices with fields open, high, low, close, volume, adj_close, date and frequency, plus adj_open/adj_high/adj_low/adj_volume "adjusted for splits and dividends" and a `factor` field ("the factor by which to multiply stock prices before this date, in order to calculate historically-adjusted stock prices"). | [Stock Prices by Security — API Documentation \| Intrinio](https://docs.intrinio.com/documentation/web_api/get_security_stock_prices_v2) | 2026-08-26 | Answers "does Intrinio publish prices, or is it quote-only?" — it publishes full OHLCV bar data, not just quotes, with daily/weekly/monthly/quarterly/yearly frequencies. The docs page does not state a history depth limit, so the actual available start date per security is unknown from this page. The presence of a `factor` field means the caller must handle adjustment explicitly for PIT correctness. |
| `PSR-EST-009` | Intrinio | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Intrinio describes the endpoint https://api-v2.intrinio.com/zacks/eps_estimates as: "This database offers consensus earnings estimates for over 5,000 US and Canadian listed companies." | [Zacks EPS Estimates — API Documentation \| Intrinio](https://docs.intrinio.com/documentation/web_api/get_zacks_eps_estimates_v2) | 2026-08-26 | Establishes universe size and that the underlying source is Zacks. Says nothing about history depth, snapshot cadence, or point-in-time integrity. Coverage is US/Canada only — acceptable for a US-equity system but noted. |
| `PSR-EST-010` | Intrinio | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The only date-typed field in the Zacks EPS Estimates response object is `date`, documented as "The period end date"; the remaining period fields are fiscal_year, fiscal_quarter, calendar_year and calendar_quarter, and the estimate fields are count, mean, median, high, low, standard_deviation and percent_change. | [Zacks EPS Estimates — API Documentation \| Intrinio](https://docs.intrinio.com/documentation/web_api/get_zacks_eps_estimates_v2) | 2026-08-26 | ANSWERS THE CRITICAL QUESTION IN THE NEGATIVE. There is no as_of, snapshot_date, updated_at, effective_date or revision field anywhere in the documented response. `date` is the fiscal period end, not the date the consensus was measured. Caveat: the docs page is JS-rendered and the fetch returned a condensed field list, so a field could in principle be present in the live schema but absent from the rendered documentation — see the corroborating SDK record. |
| `PSR-EST-011` | Intrinio | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The only backward-looking consensus values exposed are four fixed lookbacks — mean_7_days_ago, mean_30_days_ago, mean_60_days_ago and mean_90_days_ago — attached to the current record. | [Zacks EPS Estimates — API Documentation \| Intrinio](https://docs.intrinio.com/documentation/web_api/get_zacks_eps_estimates_v2) | 2026-08-26 | This is a revision-momentum convenience, not point-in-time history: it gives four scalars relative to TODAY, for the CURRENT fiscal period only. It cannot reconstruct what consensus stood on an arbitrary past date, cannot reconstruct consensus for a period that has since been reported, and if a historical row is not retained the lookbacks vanish with it. Insufficient for survivorship-free backtesting of a revision factor. |
| `PSR-EST-012` | Intrinio | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The Zacks EPS Surprises response includes eps_mean_estimate ("The pre-earnings release mean EPS estimate"), eps_count_estimate ("The pre-earnings release number of estimates"), eps_std_dev_estimate, eps_actual, eps_amount_diff, eps_percent_diff, and actual_reported_date/actual_reported_time. | [Zacks EPS Surprises — API Documentation \| Intrinio](https://docs.intrinio.com/documentation/web_api/get_zacks_eps_surprises_v2) | 2026-08-26 | This is the ONE genuinely historical, correctly-dated consensus value the Zacks-via-Intrinio route exposes: the consensus immediately before each earnings release, anchored to actual_reported_date. But it is exactly ONE observation per company per fiscal quarter. It supports PEAD-style surprise work; it CANNOT support a continuous revision-history or as-of-any-date consensus series. The documentation does not state the exact cut-off convention used to freeze the "pre-earnings release" consensus. |

Two further caveats: (a) the SDK docs describe mean_7/30/60/90_days_ago as "long term growth mean estimate" figures, which reads as a copy-paste error from the growth-rates model and leaves their exact semantics ambiguous; (b) an SDK model reflects the schema at the pinned SDK version, not necessarily today's live API. |

"Custom access" means even at Enterprise the estimates entitlement is negotiated, so no bounded cost estimate for estimates data can be given from public sources. This lists only the UNADJUSTED variants; it confirms the Detail/Summary/Actuals/Excluded structure but does not enumerate the recommendations or guidance tables. |
| `PSR-EST-028` | WRDS (Wharton Research Data Services) | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The IBES Detail History estimate record carries, per row, the fields TICKER, ESTDATS (estimate date), BROKER, ANALYS (analyst code), VALUE, FPEDATS (fiscal period end date), REVDATS (revision date) and REPDATS (report date of earnings) — shown in worked examples and in SAS code ("keep=ticker fpedats measure value usfirm estdats broker revdats analys fpi"). | [A Note on IBES Unadjusted Data (Robinson & Glushkov, WRDS, November 2006)](https://wrds-www.wharton.upenn.edu/documents/5/A_Note_on_IBES_Unadjusted_Data_pdf.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | THE key structural evidence: individual analyst-level rows carry both an estimate date and a separate revision date, so as-of reconstruction at analyst level is possible in principle. However these are DATES, not intraday timestamps — this document contains no ANNTIMS/REVTIMS/ACTTIMS time-of-day field, so sub-daily as-of precision is NOT established here. Document is from 2006. |
| `PSR-EST-029` | WRDS (Wharton Research Data Services) | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "IBES only provides unadjusted data for the EPS measure. Matters because split-adjusted estimate history introduces the rounding/mismatch problems the same document details at length — a real PIT-fidelity hazard for any surprise or revision calculation that mixes estimates and actuals across a split date. |
| `PSR-EST-030` | WRDS (Wharton Research Data Services) | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | WRDS documents that naively merging Detail History Estimates with Detail History Actuals by ticker, period end date and periodicity "will result in mismatches" around split dates, because "unadjusted analyst estimates are based on the number of shares outstanding as of the Estimate Date, while the actual reported earnings are based on the number of shares outstanding as of the Report Date", and that "surprises based on these estimates and actuals will be wrong in cases when there is a mismatch." | [A Note on IBES Unadjusted Data (Robinson & Glushkov, WRDS, November 2006)](https://wrds-www.wharton.upenn.edu/documents/5/A_Note_on_IBES_Unadjusted_Data_pdf.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | Concerns the unadjusted files specifically and is dated 2006. Nonetheless it is direct vendor-side evidence that computing a correct historical earnings surprise from I/B/E/S requires explicit split-factor handling; it is not a turnkey field. |

Critically, MONTHLY snapshots are far too coarse for a 2–30 trading-day swing horizon: a monthly consensus snapshot cannot support daily as-of reconstruction or revision-momentum signals without interpolation. The guide does not state which day of the month the snapshot is taken. |

The 1983 US start CONFLICTS with LSEG's catalogue claim of "United States from 1976"; the likely reconciliation is 1976 = summary/consensus, 1983 = analyst-level detail, but no retrieved vendor page states that reconciliation explicitly. "Daily records" is the guide's characterisation, not a quoted vendor specification. |

What IS on the public pages is data-quality disclaiming only ("indicative only and is subject to change", "for illustrative purposes only"), plus KB 2024's own framing of the feed as a "Public FTP" open to "interested parties". Two things follow for planning: (a) do not assume redistribution rights; (b) the Orbisa dashboard is a paid third-party subscription and will carry its own vendor terms that were not located publicly. Before any external use, the governing text must be read in the executed IBKR client and market-data agreements, not inferred from marketing pages. |

FAQ 104941049 documents the click path and the "Historical Rates" button; KB 2024 says the view shows prior-10-day daily min/max/mean; the IB U.K. page says historical rates can be downloaded as CSV. Those three are consistent but come from pages of differing age, and no public screenshot or schema was found. Treat "10 days of daily min/max/mean, CSV-exportable, per symbol" as the documented expectation to be confirmed in-product, not as verified UI behaviour. |

## BRW — Borrow / securities finance

| claim_id | provider | type | grade | claim | source | accessed | qualification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PSR-BRW-001` | Benzinga | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The Benzinga earnings endpoint returns a time field whose response example is rendered as "16:00:00" in HH:MM:SS form, while the same documentation's OpenAPI schema gives the example value "amc". | [Earnings — Benzinga API Documentation](https://docs.benzinga.com/api-reference/calendar-api/get-earnings) | 2026-08-26 | THE ONE PROVIDER FOUND THAT MAY EXPOSE A CLOCK TIME — but the documentation is internally inconsistent, showing an HH:MM:SS value in the response example and a coarse "amc" token in the schema example, so the field may be polymorphic or may vary by record. Retrieved directly from the vendor's own docs. The docs give NO textual description of the field, NO timezone statement, and NO indication whether the time is the scheduled/expected time or an observed actual release time — on balance it reads as scheduled. "16:00:00" is exactly the market close, which is characteristic of a default placeholder for after-close rather than a measured timestamp. Empirical validation against real responses would be required before relying on it. |
| `PSR-BRW-002` | Benzinga | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The Benzinga earnings response includes a date_confirmed field alongside date, time, updated, importance, and the EPS/revenue estimate and surprise fields. | [Earnings — Benzinga API Documentation](https://docs.benzinga.com/api-reference/calendar-api/get-earnings) | 2026-08-26 | A date_confirmed flag would let a PEAD pipeline distinguish scheduled-and-confirmed events from projections, which is the same distinction WSH sells. However the docs provide no description of the field, so its type (boolean vs date) and semantics are unresolved — the field list was enumerated from the page but individual field descriptions were absent. The presence of an updated field suggests records are revised in place, with no indication that prior values are retained for point-in-time reconstruction. |
| `PSR-BRW-003` | EquiLend | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | EquiLend Real-Time Data provides intraday securities finance transaction details including security name, ticket size, rate level and collateral type, accessible via the NGT web UI, DataLend's client platform, and a direct API link. | [EquiLend Real-Time Data](https://equilend.com/services/equilend-real-time-data/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from EquiLend's own page. This is a REAL-TIME transaction feed, not a historical research dataset — the page makes no statement at all about historical availability or depth, and none about pricing. Relevant only as evidence of what EquiLend sells alongside DataLend; not a candidate for historical backtesting. |
| `PSR-BRW-004` | EquiLend / DataLend | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | DataLend provides "volume-weighted average fees, utilization, on-loan and inventory balances, short interest, re-rate information, transaction-level data, corporate actions" and describes both "current and historical data". | [Global Securities Finance Data — DataLend](https://datalend.com/services/datalend/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from DataLend's own service page. The field set is exactly what a borrow model needs (fees + utilization + inventory). But "current and historical" is entirely unquantified — the page names no start year and no retention window. It also renders a "Unique Securities" counter as 0, i.e. a broken/placeholder statistic, so no coverage count can be taken from this page. |
| `PSR-BRW-005` | EquiLend / DataLend | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | DataLend delivers via four channels: raw data files, a web-based user interface, an Excel Add-In, and an API. | [Global Securities Finance Data — DataLend](https://datalend.com/services/datalend/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from DataLend's own page. Confirms an API exists. The page gives no API documentation, no endpoint list, no authentication model, no rate limits and no statement of how much history the API exposes — none of that is public. Coverage is described only qualitatively as "all asset classes, regions and markets globally". |
| `PSR-BRW-006` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | ORTEX documents distinct endpoints for Cost to Borrow (all loans and new loans), Short Availability, Days to Cover, Short Interest (including intraday and live), and Short Score, at per-stock, per-index and universe-wide scopes. | [ORTEX APIs documentation index (llms.txt)](https://docs.ortex.com/llms.txt) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own machine-readable docs index. This establishes the BREADTH of the short/borrow field set, which is genuinely wide. It establishes nothing about historical depth, credit cost, or which subscription tier each endpoint requires — none of those appear in the index. |
| `PSR-BRW-007` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The index-level endpoint GET /api/v1/index/short_ctb takes a single "date" parameter ("Date for shorts data (defaults to last available date)") and returns a single-date snapshot for the constituents of one index, not a time series. | [Cost To Borrow (index) — ORTEX APIs reference](https://docs.ortex.com/reference/index_short_ctb_list) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API reference. This is a structural constraint that matters more than price: the multi-name endpoint is SNAPSHOT-per-date, so assembling a historical cross-sectional panel requires one call per trading day. Index choice is also restricted (Europe Top 600, UK Top 100, US-N 100, US-S 500; default US-S 500) — that is not a broad U.S. universe. No statement on whether historical dates are accepted or how far back. |
| `PSR-BRW-008` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The universe-wide endpoint /api/v1/short_interest/ctb/all/ takes a single "date" parameter plus an optional region filter (USCanada, AsiaPac, AME, LATAM, Europe, ISO country codes, or "All") and returns a single-date universe snapshot. | [Cost to Borrow - All Loans (universe) — ORTEX APIs reference](https://docs.ortex.com/reference/short_interest_ctb_all_list.md) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API reference. Same structural constraint as the index endpoint: no from_date/to_date, so a multi-year universe panel means one call per trading day (~250/yr, ~1,750 for seven years) with undocumented credit cost per call. The reference also says full access requires contacting sales@ortex.com and that the public TEST key returns "a list of randomly selected rows" — i.e. the free key cannot be used to measure real coverage or depth. |
| `PSR-BRW-009` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The per-stock endpoint GET /api/v1/stock/{exchange_symbol}/{ticker}/availability accepts from_date/to_date and returns dated rows containing shortAvailabilityShares ("Estimated number of shares available to borrow") and shortAvailabilityPct. | [Short Availability — ORTEX APIs reference](https://docs.ortex.com/reference/stock_availability_list.md) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API reference. This is the borrow-AVAILABILITY analogue of the CTB endpoint and is likewise date-ranged and per-ticker. The values are described as ESTIMATES, not broker-confirmed locate inventory — they are not equivalent to an IB shortstock availability file. No stated historical floor. Untested (no key used). |
| `PSR-BRW-010` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The per-stock endpoint GET /api/v1/stock/{exchange_symbol}/{ticker}/ctb/all accepts from_date and to_date parameters ("Starting date for short interest data (defaults to one month ago). Format: YYYY-MM-DD") and returns a historical time series of cost-to-borrow. | [Cost To Borrow - All — ORTEX APIs reference](https://docs.ortex.com/reference/stock_ctb_all_list.md) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API reference. This CONFIRMS date-ranged historical CTB retrieval exists per ticker. It does NOT establish how far back from_date may validly go — the reference states no minimum date, no retention policy and no tier-based history cap. Retrieving one ticker at a time is also the wrong shape for a universe backfill. I did not exercise the API (no key was requested or used), so the practical depth is untested. |
| `PSR-BRW-011` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The ORTEX API "Trader" tier is $49/month, includes 1,000 Credits, gives "Single Stock APIs" only, and has a rate limit of 1 req/s. | [Subscribe to API Data — ORTEX](https://ortex.com/pay/api/start_subscription_page/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API subscription page. Decisive limitation for broad-universe work: Trader is single-stock only, so a whole-market historical panel is not reachable at this tier. The page does not define what one credit buys (per call? per row? per date returned?), so 1,000 credits cannot be converted into a usable data volume. |
| `PSR-BRW-012` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The ORTEX API "Quant" tier is $149/month, includes 10,000 Credits, gives "Access to all APIs including bulk endpoints", and has a rate limit of 5 req/s. | [Subscribe to API Data — ORTEX](https://ortex.com/pay/api/start_subscription_page/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API subscription page. This is the cheapest tier that unlocks full-universe bulk endpoints and is therefore the most relevant price point for a broad-universe historical borrow panel. Credit definition is still undocumented, so whether 10,000 credits covers a multi-year universe backfill is UNKNOWN and is the main cost risk. No historical-depth statement on this page. |
| `PSR-BRW-013` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The ORTEX API "Developer" tier is listed at $499/month with "50% off first month" ($249.50 first month), includes 100,000 Credits and a 50 req/s rate limit. | [Subscribe to API Data — ORTEX](https://ortex.com/pay/api/start_subscription_page/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own API subscription page. The $249.50 figure is a promotional first-month price, not the recurring rate; recurring is $499/month. Promotional pricing is inherently time-sensitive and may not persist. Credit semantics undocumented as with the other tiers. |
| `PSR-BRW-014` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The ORTEX Basic plan includes "Delayed short interest, cost to borrow & days to cover". | [ORTEX · Live Short Interest Data & Analytics](https://public.ortex.com/) | 2026-08-26 | Verbatim from the plan card on ORTEX's own home page. "Delayed" is not quantified anywhere on the page — the lag length is unstated. The card does not mention utilization or shares-available for Basic, so their inclusion at this tier is unestablished. Says nothing about historical depth. |
| `PSR-BRW-015` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The ORTEX Advanced plan includes "Real-time short interest & cost to borrow" and states "API access included". | [ORTEX · Live Short Interest Data & Analytics](https://public.ortex.com/) | 2026-08-26 | Verbatim from the home-page plan card. "API access included" is ambiguous and is NOT the same as the separately-sold API subscription tiers (Trader/Quant/Developer) that ORTEX prices on its API subscription page — the relationship between the bundled access and those paid tiers, including any credit allowance, is not stated anywhere I could retrieve. Treat API entitlement as unresolved. |
| `PSR-BRW-016` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | ORTEX's home page advertises a backtester with the example "Backtest 4 Jan 2010 to 25 Aug 2026 (16.6 years), top-ranked S&P 500 names, monthly rebalance" and references a "16-year backtest". | [ORTEX · Live Short Interest Data & Analytics (backtester section)](https://public.ortex.com/) | 2026-08-26 | IMPORTANT — DO NOT READ THIS AS BORROW-DATA DEPTH. The 2010-2026 range describes ORTEX's Stock SCORES backtester ("One daily score per stock, built from hundreds of data points"), not the securities-lending/CTB series. ORTEX's own securities-lending sourcing narrative elsewhere is much more recent. Treating this 16.6-year figure as cost-to-borrow history would be a material error. The same page separately cites "5yr daily history" for Index Rebalance data, showing depth varies per dataset. |
| `PSR-BRW-017` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | An ORTEX article dated 31 May 2022 announced a Free tier plus Basic at $39/month ($348/year) and Advanced at $129/month ($948/year). | [A New Pricing Structure for ORTEX](https://public.ortex.com/articles/a-new-pricing-structure-for-ortex) | 2026-08-26 | OBSOLETE — retained only to explain a discrepancy. Several search-engine summaries still surface these 2022 numbers as current; the live pricing page today shows $49/$149 monthly and $39/$99 annual-equivalent. Cite the live page, not this. Does confirm a Free tier existed in 2022 with "delayed short interest", which the current home page no longer clearly enumerates. |
| `PSR-BRW-018` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | ORTEX exposes Short Interest & Securities Lending data — shares shorted, percentage of free float, USD value, availability, borrowing costs, days to cover and regulatory disclosures — via both API and an Excel Add-in. | [APIs and Excel Add-In — ORTEX](https://public.ortex.com/articles/apis-and-excel-add-in) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own article. Multi-currency (EUR/GBP/CAD/SEK) variants listed; USD quoted here. Not dated on-page, so I cannot prove when it was last changed. |
| `PSR-BRW-021` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | ORTEX Advanced is $149 USD/month billed monthly, or $99 USD/month billed annually. | [Pricing — ORTEX](https://public.ortex.com/ortex-pricing/) | 2026-08-26 | Same page and same limitation as the Basic price record: price only, no feature breakdown on this page. | `PSR-BRW-022` | ORTEX | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | ORTEX sources securities-lending data from "over 700k pools of liquidity" across Agent Lenders, Prime Brokers and Broker-Dealers, updated by 7:30am EST each day, and derives Cost to Borrow as the average interest rate for current loans. | [ORTEX Short Interest Data (methodology article)](https://public.ortex.com/ortex-short-interest-data/) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from ORTEX's own methodology article. Establishes that ORTEX CTB is a MODELLED/aggregated lending-market rate, not a broker's own charged rate — it will not reproduce IBKR's account-level borrow fee, which matters if backtests are to be reconciled against actual IB execution costs. The article states no historical depth and does not mention point-in-time or backtesting at all. |

Retrieved from the vendor-authored AWS Marketplace listing (AWS controls the pricing widget), not from s3partners.com. I did NOT subscribe — prohibited by task constraints — so I cannot confirm whether the subscription requires vendor approval, is evaluation-scoped, or whether the free entitlement includes the full historical file or only recent data. AWS Data Exchange free listings commonly gate on seller approval. The listing also notes "Additional AWS infrastructure costs may apply" (S3 storage/egress). Treat as a promising lead requiring confirmation, not an established free source. |
| `PSR-BRW-024` | S3 Partners | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The listing states "Time period coverage: Since 2015" and delivers two datasets — a "Daily Data File" and a "Historical Data File" — both including "All historical revisions" and "All future revisions". | [S3 Revised Short Interest & Securities Financing Data — AWS Marketplace / AWS Data Exchange](https://aws.amazon.com/marketplace/pp/prodview-l3a2kf5yecuy6) | 2026-08-26 | Retrieved from the vendor-authored AWS listing. ~11 years of history with explicit revision tracking, which is the closest thing to point-in-time discipline found at a non-institutional price. Caveat: "all historical revisions" describes revision lineage, which is necessary but not sufficient for true point-in-time reconstruction — the as-of semantics are not documented on the listing. Update frequency stated as "Hourly (Morning 8AM is SOD data and then hourly updates from 9AM - 5PM ET)". Whether the free subscription actually grants the Historical Data File is untested. |

## MISC — Unrouted

| claim_id | provider | type | grade | claim | source | accessed | qualification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PSR-MISC-001` | EarningsAPI | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The EarningsAPI calendar response has no timing field at all; it groups companies into three arrays named pre, after, and notSupplied, alongside a date string in YYYY-MM-DD format. | [Earnings Calendar API — Earnings API Documentation](https://www.earningsapi.com/docs/calendar-earnings) | 2026-08-26 | A minor third-party provider, included only as a further datapoint that the coarse-bucket pattern is industry-wide. Retrieved directly from the vendor's docs. The shape (grouping arrays rather than a per-row flag) differs from every other provider examined, so it is not drop-in compatible. Provider scale, coverage, history depth, reliability and pricing were not assessed and no independent evidence of data quality was sought. |
| `PSR-MISC-002` | Stefano DellaVigna and Joshua Pollet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | "Since the time stamp for each announcement is not available in I/B/E/S or Compustat, we cannot separate announcements made during trading hours from those made after close." | [Investor Inattention and Friday Earnings Announcements (working paper, author-hosted)](https://eml.berkeley.edu/~sdellavi/wp/earnfr06-12-11NewTitle.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | STRONG SUPPORT FOR THE PLAN'S CONSERVATIVE STANCE: the two standard institutional research databases carry no announcement timestamp at all, forcing published researchers to abandon the intraday-versus-after-close split entirely. Retrieved as a PDF from the author's own Berkeley page and extracted verbatim with pdftotext. This is a working-paper version, not the final Journal of Finance published text, and the sample period ends in 2004 — commercial timestamp availability has improved since (see Benzinga and the WSH EPS actual_date1 field), so this understates what is obtainable today at a price. It speaks to I/B/E/S and Compustat only, not to SEC filing timestamps. |
| `PSR-MISC-003` | Stefano DellaVigna and Joshua Pollet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | After validating imputed announcement dates against Lexis-Nexis PR newswire searches on 2,601 randomly selected announcements, the authors report that for the post-1994 period "the imputed earnings announcement date is correct more than 95 percent of the time for both Friday and non-Friday announcements", while "Before 1995, a high number of earnings announcements were recorded with an error of at least one trading day." | [Investor Inattention and Friday Earnings Announcements (working paper, author-hosted)](https://eml.berkeley.edu/~sdellavi/wp/earnfr06-12-11NewTitle.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | QUANTIFIES THE RESIDUAL DATE RISK: even in the clean modern period, roughly 1 in 20 events carries a DATE error of at least one trading day — before any question of time-of-day. This is a direct argument for the plan's conservative next-session-open lag, since a one-day date error plus an aggressive same-session entry assumption compounds into trading the wrong session. Caveats: the >95% figure applies to the authors' own imputation rule combining I/B/E/S and Compustat, not to any single vendor feed and not to WSH or SEC filings; the measurement period ends in 2004 and accuracy has likely improved; and the appendix accuracy table extracted cleanly for 1984-1994 panels but the post-1994 panel was not read directly, so the >95% figure comes from the body text. |
| `PSR-MISC-004` | Stefano DellaVigna and Joshua Pollet | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | When I/B/E/S and Compustat announcement dates disagree, "the earlier date is usually the actual date of the announcement, and the later date is the date of publication in the Wall Street Journal." | [Investor Inattention and Friday Earnings Announcements (working paper, author-hosted)](https://eml.berkeley.edu/~sdellavi/wp/earnfr06-12-11NewTitle.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | A concrete, implementable reconciliation rule if the plan ever cross-checks two date vendors — take the earlier date on disagreement. Derived from a 1984-2003 sample in which Wall Street Journal publication dates contaminated the databases; that specific contamination mechanism is largely historical, so the rule's rationale may not transfer to modern vendor pairs even if the heuristic still holds. Working-paper version. |
| `PSR-MISC-005` | TMX Datalinx Webstore | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | DateBreaks "tracks earnings date changes from the past two US business days for any traded company that is tracked by Wall Street Horizon (WSH)", and "The file is produced every five (5) minutes beginning at 5:00am ET and ending at 11:00pm ET" in XML delivered by FTP. | [Wall Street Horizon (WSH) DateBreaks \| TMX Datalinx Webstore](https://www.tmxwebstore.com/products/wsh-datebreaks) | 2026-08-26 | KEY LIMITATION FOR POINT-IN-TIME USE: the file window is only the trailing two US business days. DateBreaks is a rolling change feed, NOT a queryable archive — building a multi-year revision history requires capturing and storing every five-minute file continuously from the start, or buying a separate history product. Covers earnings-date changes only, not changes to any other event type. |
| `PSR-MISC-006` | TMX Datalinx Webstore | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | DateBreaks includes a value-add field "Confirmed Date Z-Score" giving "insight into whether confirmed earnings dates align with historical trends", across "over 30 corporate event classes for over 9,300 of largest market capitalization and most heavily traded stocks worldwide." | [Wall Street Horizon (WSH) DateBreaks \| TMX Datalinx Webstore](https://www.tmxwebstore.com/products/wsh-datebreaks) | 2026-08-26 | The Z-score's construction, window, and normalization are not documented on this page, so it cannot be reproduced or validated from public information — it is an opaque vendor score. The 30-event-class/9,300-company figure describes WSH's overall tracked universe, not the DateBreaks file specifically. No DateBreaks record layout or field list was publicly retrievable. |

Once-daily delivery. "time of day" is presumably the same four-value categorical as the other WSH products but this page does not enumerate the values. The page gives no field-level specification and no price — access is quote-driven at this tier. "API" is asserted here but the retrieved product user guides describe FTP file delivery only; the API's existence, endpoints and schema were not verified. |

Included because it is the only quantified filing-interval evidence located, and it is a useful reminder that the audited financials trail the release by roughly two weeks. It does NOT bear on whether 8-K acceptance time approximates the press release. Vendor blog post using 2017 data, annual filings only; methodology and sample size are not stated. |

Every figure here (6,500 companies, 2006 start date, field list) should be re-confirmed with Cboe before being relied on. If accurate this is the most promising point-in-time source found: daily snapshots back to 2006 constitute a genuine as-of revision history suitable for backtesting, and unlike the DateBreaks feed it is purchasable as history rather than requiring live capture. Note "time of day" is listed as a field but the summary does not give its allowed values — on the evidence of every other WSH product it is the four-value categorical, not a clock time. No price was obtainable. |
| `PSR-MISC-012` | Ed deHaan, Terry Shevlin and Jacob Thornock (via search-index summaries) | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | The authors find evidence consistent with managers reporting bad news after market hours, on busy days, and with less advance notice, with earnings receiving less attention in those settings. | [Market (in)attention and the strategic scheduling and timing of earnings announcements, Journal of Accounting and Economics 60(1), 2015](https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=2545966) | 2026-08-26 | NOT DIRECTLY RETRIEVED — recorded from search-index summaries of the SSRN abstract and related pages; the paper itself was not fetched and the published JAE version is paywalled. Relevant to the plan as a WARNING that announcement timing is ENDOGENOUS: firms choose after-hours and low-attention slots strategically and correlate that choice with bad news, so timing classification error is not random noise but is correlated with the sign of the surprise — which can bias a PEAD study. It contains no information about data-source field availability or timestamp precision. |
| `PSR-MISC-013` | Latham & Watkins | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | A filing transmission that begins before 5:30 p.m. ET is deemed filed that same day, whereas one that begins after 5:30 p.m. ET is deemed filed on the next business day, even though EDGAR accepts it that day. | [How does EDGAR's 5:30 pm filing cutoff work?](https://wow.lw.com/Article/Index/186) | 2026-08-26 | DECISIVE FOR IMPLEMENTATION: the plan must key on EDGAR's ACCEPTANCE datetime, never the FILING DATE. Because a large share of earnings releases are after the close, their 8-Ks are commonly transmitted after 5:30 p.m. and carry a filing date one business day LATER than the actual event — silently shifting the event by a full session and corrupting PEAD alignment. A law-firm explainer (secondary); the authoritative EDGAR Filer Manual on sec.gov returned 403. The article is undated on its face, so confirm the 5:30 p.m. cutoff is current. It also notes filings must be completed by 10:00 p.m. to be accepted that day. |
| `PSR-MISC-014` | M2 Compliance | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | "Filing of earnings press releases (Item 2.02(b)) must be completed before any associated analyst conference call." | [Frequently Asked Questions about Form 8-K](https://www.m2compliance.com/8k-form-faq.php) | 2026-08-26 | Supplies a PRACTICAL UPPER BOUND that is much tighter than the four-business-day statutory allowance: because most issuers hold the call within roughly an hour of an after-close release, the 8-K is in practice pushed out within that window. But this constraint binds only when the issuer wants the nonwritten-communications safe harbour for the call — an issuer holding no call, or forgoing the exception, is not bound by it. A filing-agent's FAQ, not the SEC; undated; the underlying SEC rule text could not be retrieved directly (sec.gov 403). |
| `PSR-MISC-015` | Nasdaq (via search-index summaries) | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | The Nasdaq earnings calendar classifies report timing as "Pre-Market", "After Hours", or "Time Not Supplied", with the underlying JSON reportedly using the token values "time-pre-market", "time-after-hours" and "time-not-supplied". | [Nasdaq earnings calendar timing labels (search-index summary)](https://www.nasdaq.com/market-activity/earnings) | 2026-08-26 | NOT DIRECTLY VERIFIED — Nasdaq's own page and API both timed out (paired U record). Derived from search-engine summaries and third-party wrapper descriptions, one of which (a Grokipedia page) is a generative encyclopedia and not authoritative. The substantive point for the plan is nonetheless consistent across every provider examined: a three-way coarse bucket with an explicit unknown state, and no clock time. The presence of an explicit "not supplied" token is itself evidence that timing is frequently absent. |
| `PSR-MISC-016` | Skadden, Arps, Slate, Meagher & Flom LLP | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | "Under Item 2.02 of Form 8-K, a report on Form 8-K is not required to be furnished or filed until four business days after the initial disclosure." | [A Primer On Reg. FD And The Earnings Release Process (Michael Zeidel)](https://www.skadden.com/-/media/files/publications/2013/12/a-primer-on-reg-fd-and-the-earnings-release-proces.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | EVIDENCE AGAINST RELYING SOLELY ON 8-K ACCEPTANCE TIME. This establishes the legal WORST CASE: the 8-K may lawfully lag the press release by up to four business days, so 8-K acceptance time is an upper bound on the announcement time and can be badly wrong in the tail. A law-firm client publication (secondary), retrieved as a PDF and extracted locally with pdftotext; the underlying SEC rule text on sec.gov could not be retrieved directly. Dated December 2013 and excerpted from Lexis Practice Advisor — verify the rule is unchanged. It does not quantify how often the four-day allowance is actually used. |
| `PSR-MISC-017` | Skadden, Arps, Slate, Meagher & Flom LLP | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | "In practice, companies typically issue their earnings releases through a newswire service simultaneously with the filing (or furnishing) of the related Form 8-K and hold their earnings call within 48 hours after the release." | [A Primer On Reg. FD And The Earnings Release Process (Michael Zeidel)](https://www.skadden.com/-/media/files/publications/2013/12/a-primer-on-reg-fd-and-the-earnings-release-proces.pdf) | 2026-08-26 | EVIDENCE IN FAVOUR OF THE PLAN'S 8-K APPROACH, and the single most directly on-point statement located about the press-release-to-8-K gap. But "typically" and "simultaneously" are qualitative — this source provides NO distribution, median, or tail quantification of the gap, which is what the plan actually needs. Note the driver is Regulation FD's simultaneous-disclosure requirement, which pushes toward co-timing, while the Item 2.02 four-business-day allowance permits lag; practice sits between the two. Law-firm secondary source, December 2013. |

| `PSR-MISC-019` | TMX Datalinx Webstore | `OFFICIAL` | **`Q`** | No price is displayed for the WSH Earnings Calendar; the page states "Please sign in with your TMX Datalinx Webstore account to view pricing information." | [Wall Street Horizon (WSH) Earnings Calendar \| TMX Datalinx Webstore](https://www.tmxwebstore.com/products/wsh-earnings-calendar) | 2026-08-26 | Graded Q/OFFICIAL because the vendor's own page explicitly gates the price behind an account rather than omitting it. This means a price DOES exist in a self-serve catalogue — unlike a pure enterprise quote — but it was not retrievable without registering, which the task prohibits. The sibling page https://datashop.cboe.com/data-products was also fetched and likewise showed no Wall Street Horizon entries and no prices. No login was attempted. Not investigated in depth: Visible Alpha, S&P Global Market Intelligence / Capital IQ Estimates, Estimize's current commercial status, Benzinga/Polygon analyst-rating revision history, and academic PIT reconstructions. The unauthenticated JSON endpoint https://api.nasdaq.com/api/calendar/earnings was also attempted twice (dates 2026-08-26 and 2026-08-25) and timed out both times — consistent with Nasdaq's edge blocking non-browser clients. Nasdaq publishes no formal developer documentation for this calendar endpoint that could be located, so even a successful fetch would have yielded an observed payload rather than a documented schema. |
