## R2 — 2026-08-27 re-verification round (G1 / G3 decision packet)

A second research round, performed **2026-08-27** for
[provider-licensing-decision-packet.md](provider-licensing-decision-packet.md). It is the
maintenance rule below, exercised: rows are re-verified, and several that were `U` on
2026-08-26 are now answered. Where a row here supersedes an earlier one, the earlier row is
named. **Nothing was purchased, trialled or credentialed, no vendor account exists, no API key
was requested or used, and no vendor data was retrieved** — including the publicly advertised
sample and test key recorded in `PSR-SHD-103`, which were read *about* and not called.

| claim_id | provider | type | grade | claim | source | accessed | qualification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PSR-SHD-081` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §5 COMPLIANCE AND ENFORCEMENT, in full: "You will only use the Services and the Services Data as explicitly permitted by this Personal Use License. Sharadar may request reasonable proof that you are using this License appropriately (for example, that you are an individual using the data for personal purposes only). Any use outside the scope of personal, non-professional use by a natural person—including professional, institutional, or entity use—may result in immediate cancellation of your access to the Services and the Services Data without refund, at Sharadar's sole discretion." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §5 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | **Answers the gap flagged in `PSR-SHD-064`**, which recorded §5 as unread and possibly containing audit rights. It does: a right to "request reasonable proof". It is a proof-on-request right, not an inspection or systems-audit right, and no notice period, evidence standard or appeal is stated. Enforcement is cancellation without refund at sole discretion. Model-mediated quote. |
| `PSR-SHD-082` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §18 MODIFICATIONS AND UPDATES: "Sharadar reserves the right to change or modify any of the terms and conditions contained in these License Terms at any time and in its sole discretion. Any changes or modification will be effective upon posting of the revisions and notice of such changes to the Sharadar's website." and "Your continued use of the Services or Services Data following the posting of the changes or modifications will constitute your acceptance of such changes or modifications." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §18 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | **Answers the second gap flagged in `PSR-SHD-064`.** Unilateral amendment, effective on posting, accepted by continued use. Note the clause does provide for **notice**: changes take effect on posting of the revisions **and notice of such changes to the website**, so amendment is not silent by design. What public research did **not** establish is **where** those notices appear, how long they remain available, or whether superseded licence revisions can be retrieved — and `PSR-SHD-089` records that the Terms page itself carries no version or effective date. The supportable conclusion is therefore narrower than "undetectable": **there is currently no durable way to pin which licence revision governed a given historical research run.** Material to any permission this project relies on, and relevant to the FAQ-versus-Terms problem in `PSR-SHD-014`, which it does not by itself resolve. Model-mediated quote. |
| `PSR-SHD-083` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §10 TERMINATION deletion obligation: "Within thirty (30) days of termination, delete from all computer systems you own or operate all copies of the Services Data (including downloads, bulk files, caches, and extracts), all data sets that contain, substantially copy, or could reproduce the Services Data or Sharadar tables, and all software provided as part of the Services." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §10 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | **Materially wider than `PSR-SHD-061` recorded on 2026-08-26**, which captured only "all copies of the Services Data". The parenthetical reaches caches and extracts, and the second limb reaches any dataset that "could reproduce" the vendor tables. On a plain reading that covers a bronze archive, a silver normalisation and any gold artifact from which the vendor rows are recoverable. Model-mediated quote; the boundary of "substantially copy" is not defined in the text. |
| `PSR-SHD-084` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §10 retention carve-out: "You may keep research outputs, backtest results, models, summary statistics, trade logs, and similar derived works that do not contain and cannot reproduce the Services Data or Sharadar tables." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §10 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | Re-verifies `PSR-SHD-062` and adds "or Sharadar tables" to the reproduction test. The carve-out is real but is a test about reproducibility, not about how derived a work feels: it turns on whether the vendor rows can be recovered from the artifact. A factor panel keyed per security per date is closer to the line than a scalar performance summary. Undefined in the text. Model-mediated quote. |
| `PSR-SHD-085` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §10: "Sharadar may, without prior notice, immediately terminate, limit, or suspend your access" for breach, professional use, non-payment, or malicious actions; and "If Sharadar terminates without cause, it will refund prepaid fees pro rata for the remainder of the then-current period." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §10 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | New. Termination may be immediate and without notice, which means the 30-day deletion clock in `PSR-SHD-083` can start without warning. The pro-rata refund applies only to termination *without cause*; §9 (`PSR-SHD-045`) states no refund on subscriber cancellation. Model-mediated quote. |
| `PSR-SHD-086` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §8 CONFIDENTIALITY: "The conclusions arrived at related to the value, usability or fitness for purpose, or any other attributes of the Service or the Services Data shall not be published in any way...or provided or otherwise disclosed to any outside individual or entity, without prior written approval of Sharadar." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §8 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | Re-verifies `PSR-SHD-059` and adds the limb that 2026-08-26 did not capture: the bar is not only on **publishing** but on **disclosing to any outside individual or entity**. That reaches private disclosure — a reviewer, a collaborator, or a third-party service — not just a public post. "Prior written approval" is confirmed as the cure, consistent with `PSR-SHD-060`. Model-mediated quote. |
| `PSR-SHD-087` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | §4 REDISTRIBUTION: "You may not transfer or make available access to either the Services or the Service Data to others. You may not publish, disseminate, re-distribute or share the Services Data, or any part thereof, or any derivative data that allows reverse engineering." | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License, §4 \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | Adds a limb `PSR-SHD-055` did not capture: "or any derivative data that allows reverse engineering". This is the redistribution-side analogue of the §10 reproduction test and points the same way — a derived artifact is publishable only if the vendor rows cannot be recovered from it. Model-mediated quote; "allows reverse engineering" is undefined. |
| `PSR-SHD-088` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Negative finding, from a targeted read of all eighteen sections: **no section of the Personal Use License mentions** automated access, API access limits, programmatic access, scripts, bots, rate limits, machine learning, artificial intelligence, large language models, model training, or transmitting data to a third-party service. | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | A silence, recorded as evidence. It cuts both ways and must not be read as a grant: automated retrieval is separately *described* by the vendor's own FAQ and API documentation (`PSR-SHD-107`, `PSR-SHD-102`), whereas third-party LLM submission is not described anywhere and would fall to be judged under §4 (`PSR-SHD-087`) and §8 (`PSR-SHD-086`). A targeted prompt cannot prove absence as strongly as a full verbatim read; graded `V` because all eighteen section headings were enumerated and searched. |
| `PSR-SHD-089` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The Terms page displays no "last updated", version or effective date. | [Terms & Use — Personal Use License \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | Second consecutive round with the same negative finding, corroborating `PSR-SHD-065`. Read together with `PSR-SHD-082`: terms may change unilaterally, and although §18 states that notice of changes is posted to the website, **the page itself carries no version marker against which a subscriber could identify which revision they are looking at**, and the location, persistence and retrievability of those notices were not established. The consequence is an inability to **pin a licence revision to a past research run**, not an inability to notice change at all. A footer date dropped by the fetch tool still cannot be entirely excluded. |

Investors: 5y $19/$99; 10y $29/$199; Full History $39/$299. Bundle: 5y $29/mo or $299/yr; 10y $49/mo or $399/yr; Full History $69/mo or $499/yr. | [Subscribe \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/subscribe) | 2026-08-27 | **Supersedes `PSR-SHD-080`**, which recorded annual prices as unobtainable, and **materially qualifies `PSR-SHD-042`**, which recorded "$29/mo Bundle" without the history dimension. The $29/mo Bundle is the **5-year** tier. This is a purchase-blocking ambiguity for a plan whose survivorship control needs a stated depth. |

It is also typed `date`, not timestamp, so even as a bound it is day-resolution. Graded `V2`: the wording came from a search-index summary of the vendor page; the rendered documentation page shows `lastupdated` in the schema table as "Last Updated Date" with no prose definition (`PSR-SHD-101`). Corroborates `PSR-SHD-022`. Re-verify verbatim before relying on it. |

Consequence: the contract §9 lag applies and `CORPORATE_ACTION_ANNOUNCE_APPROXIMATED` is declared on every dependent result. Caveat: the page does not state what the single `date` means per action type either (`PSR-SHD-095`), so which date it is remains unconfirmed. |
| `PSR-SHD-095` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | ACTIONS covers "each active and delisted ticker" with history from "January 1998", and the documented action types include stock splits, cash dividends, spinoffs, **ticker changes**, ADR ratio changes, listing dates, delisting dates, delist reasons and acquisition counterparties. The page does not enumerate the exact `action` code values, nor state what `date`, `value`, `contraticker` and `contraname` hold for each action type. | [Documentation — Corporate Actions \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/actions) | 2026-08-27 | Two findings in one row. The favourable half: ticker-change events exist with dates, which is the raw material from which a historical ticker mapping can be **derived** (see `PSR-SHD-096`). The unfavourable half: per-action field semantics are undocumented, so the derivation cannot be specified before access. Two fetches were made; the second, asking specifically for the enumerated action codes, returned that they are not documented. |
| `PSR-SHD-096` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The TICKERS table carries `permaticker`, "a unique and unchanging identifier for an issuer. Specific to the Sharadar database"; the current `ticker`; `isdelisted` (Y/N); and the date fields `firstadded`, `lastupdated`, `firstpricedate`, `lastpricedate`. It **does not carry a date-ranged historical ticker-symbol mapping** — the table holds the current symbol. | [Documentation — Tickers and Metadata \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/tickers) | 2026-08-27 | **Qualifies `PSR-SHD-026`**, which recorded "tickers table is time-structured — verify in 3A". On the vendor's documented schema it is not: identity is solved by `permaticker`, but ticker-at-a-date is not supplied as a dataset. This is the same structural shape that disqualified Norgate (`PSR-NRG-012`) — with one decisive difference: Norgate supplies no change log, whereas Sharadar's ACTIONS carries dated ticker-change events (`PSR-SHD-095`). Ticker history is therefore obtainable from Sharadar as a **KalpaMani-derived artifact**, not as a vendor fact, and must be recorded as such. |
| `PSR-SHD-097` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Stock prices are supplied with "3 different adjustment methods: (1) unadjusted; (2) stock-split adjusted; and, (3) stock-split adjusted, cash dividend adjusted, and spinoff adjusted", with columns including "Close Price - Unadjusted", the split-adjusted OHLC set, and "Close Price - Adjusted for Splits Dividends and Spinoffs". | [Documentation — Stock Prices \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/stocks) | 2026-08-27 | Favourable, and it makes provider test **P5** runnable: both the raw series and the vendor's adjusted series are supplied, so recomputing adjusted from raw plus ACTIONS and reconciling against the vendor's own adjusted column is a self-contained test needing no second vendor. It also satisfies the contract §8 requirement that gold store raw bars, since raw is available rather than inferred. |
| `PSR-SHD-098` | Sharadar | `UNAVAILABLE` | **`U`** | How the daily bars are constructed — officially disseminated consolidated tape, an official closing price, or aggregation by the provider from its own trade collection — is **not stated** on the stock prices documentation page, which describes delivery frequency and reporting lag but not construction or source. | [Documentation — Stock Prices \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/stocks) | 2026-08-27 | **This is provider test P9, and it is unanswerable from public documentation.** It is also unanswerable from the data itself: no sample of bars reveals whether they were disseminated or aggregated. P9 is therefore a **vendor-clarification** question, not a credentialed data test — a classification the implementation plan does not currently draw. Its consequence is the largest in the set: a `PROVIDER_AGGREGATED` answer makes `price_bar` and everything derived from it, the universe included, ineligible under `PUBLIC_PIT`. |
| `PSR-SHD-099` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Stock prices: "End-of-day (EOD)...price data for active and delisted US public stocks", history from "January 1998", "Current count of 21,000 tickers". | [Documentation — Stock Prices \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/stocks) | 2026-08-27 | Re-verifies the 1998 start recorded in `PSR-SHD-042` and adds a ticker count. Note the count is "current", so it is not the count of securities ever covered. Delisted coverage is asserted by the vendor and remains unaudited — that is provider test P2, which no documentation can discharge. |
| `PSR-SHD-100` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The EVENTS table has columns `ticker` (primary key), `date` — documented as **filing date**, typed date (YYYY-MM-DD), primary key — and `eventcodes`. The table carries **no time-of-day component** and no before/after-market timing indicator, and the `eventcodes` values are not enumerated on the page. | [Documentation — Material Corporate Events \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/events) | 2026-08-27 | **Bears directly on provider test P8 and on domain G.** A date-only 8-K derivative cannot establish whether a release preceded or followed a session, which is precisely what the PEAD event window needs. The retrieved schema shows `date` ("Date Key"), `reportperiod` and `calendardate`; no `filingdate` column was listed, leaving `PSR-SHD-078` open. |
| `PSR-SHD-102` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | API access requires a key — "In order to interact with the Sharadar API, you or your application must provide an API key" — passed as a **query-string parameter**, e.g. `https://api.sharadar.com/v1.0/data/fundamentals?api_key=test-api-key&ticker=AAPL`. "Sharing API keys is not permitted and may result in your access being revoked." **No rate limit, request quota, page-size limit, concurrent-request limit or fair-use statement is documented on the page.** | [Documentation — Authentication \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/auth) | 2026-08-27 | Two consequences. (1) The phrase "you or your application" is the API documentation's own acknowledgement that programmatic access is contemplated, which supports — without granting — the automated-use reading of `PSR-SHD-107`. (2) **A key carried in the query string is a KalpaMani security problem**: URLs reach process lists, shell history, HTTP client debug logs and exception traces. CLAUDE.md §4 forbids logging or storing credentials, so query-string redaction becomes a build requirement of any client, not an optional hardening. Absence of a documented rate limit means backfill wall-clock cannot be planned. |
| `PSR-SHD-103` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | A **free sample dataset** is published, covering the fundamentals, stock prices, corporate actions, S&P 500 constituents, tickers/metadata and daily fundamentals tables, "includes more than 150+ indicators for all 30 Dow Jones Industrial Average stocks", with "5 years history". The page exposes "Open API sample (CSV)" links carrying a test API key, and a Google Sheets example. | [Sample \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/sample) | 2026-08-27 | **Materially changes what qualification costs.** A subset of the provider tests touches table shape and internal consistency rather than breadth, and those are the ones a 30-name, 5-year sample can exercise before any purchase. It does **not** reach P2 (delisted coverage at 5/10/15 years needs delisted names and depth), P6 (needs a documented multi-step restatement) or P9 (needs a vendor statement, `PSR-SHD-098`). **Not exercised in this round.** The sample is still Services Data under the same Terms, and reading it is vendor-data retrieval — outside this task's authorization and, on the register's own rule, a step requiring authorization rather than a free action. The licence governing the sample is not separately stated; the page footer links the same Terms. |
| `PSR-SHD-104` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | Blog post dated **Monday, 27 July 2026** announcing the direct service: "Offering the same data you may have seen on Nasdaq Data Link with super easy sign-up and retrieval mechanisms"; "Institutional customers can continue to find us Nasdaq"; coverage described as "deep history and coverage (99% survivorship bias from since 1999)". | [Sharadar Launches Direct \| blog.sharadar.com](https://blog.sharadar.com/2026/07/sharadar-launches-direct.html) | 2026-08-27 | Confirms from the vendor's own words that the Nasdaq Data Link channel persists for institutional users alongside Direct, corroborating `PSR-SHD-063` and `PSR-SHD-068` at official strength. **Also records a coverage discrepancy**: the blog says "since 1999" while the documentation says January 1998 (`PSR-SHD-099`, `PSR-SHD-101`). One year of stated depth is not decorative for a survivorship control, and the two vendor statements disagree. The phrase "99% survivorship bias" is evidently a slip for "99% free of survivorship bias" and is quoted as written. |

| `PSR-SHD-106` | Sharadar | `OFFICIAL` | **`V`** | The published contact route is the email address `connect@sharadar.com`, shown in the site footer and referenced by the privacy policy's contact section. The address is published on the vendor's own site as a business contact; it is not a KalpaMani account identifier and nothing about any KalpaMani account exists to record. The absence of a named legal entity and jurisdiction is itself a finding: `PSR-SHD-064` recorded §15 JURISDICTION as unread, and the 2026-08-27 fetch of §15 returned liability-cap language rather than a governing-law clause, so **the governing law and forum of this licence remain unestablished**. The email string was published Cloudflare-obfuscated and was decoded locally. |

The page carries nine questions and is silent on rate limits, local storage, post-cancellation handling, the `lastupdated` definition, and AI/ML/LLM use, and shows no version or date. | [Documentation — Frequently Asked Questions \| sharadar.com](https://sharadar.com/docs/faqs) | 2026-08-27 | Re-verifies `PSR-SHD-014` unchanged, which is the single most important permission in this stack and the only source that addresses automated own-account trading at all. Every caveat on `PSR-SHD-014` survives and one is now sharper: `PSR-SHD-082` establishes that the Terms may be amended unilaterally, and an FAQ is weaker than terms that can move. The retrieval-methods answer is the vendor describing bulk and API retrieval as the intended mechanisms, which is evidence about automated use even though it is not a licence grant. |

Please visit www.sec.gov/developer for more developer resources and Fair Access guidelines. Please visit www.sec.gov/privacy for more information on Privacy Policy." | [SEC.gov (block response served on every path attempted)](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-search-assistance/accessing-edgar-data) | 2026-08-27 | **A better-grade finding than the bare 403s of `PSR-SEC-041` through `PSR-SEC-043`.** The body is SEC-authored text retrieved from sec.gov, so the fair-access requirement itself is now `V` even though no policy page could be read. It also identifies the failure as a **rate-threshold block**, not an authorization denial — consistent with a shared or reputation-flagged egress IP rather than with anything about this project. Three paths were tried with WebFetch and three more with a plain HTTP client carrying a descriptive User-Agent; all six returned the same page. |
| `PSR-SEC-046` | Multiple secondary sources (WebSearch survey) | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | EDGAR fair access is described consistently across secondary sources as: a declared User-Agent identifying the requester with contact information, a limit of no more than 10 requests per second per requester regardless of the number of machines used, a 403 response and a temporary IP block for requests lacking a declared User-Agent or exceeding the rate, and guidance to use efficient scripting and download only what is needed. | [SEC.gov \| Accessing EDGAR Data (via search-index summaries and third-party library documentation)](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-search-assistance/accessing-edgar-data) | 2026-08-27 | Second consecutive round in which the authoritative page could not be read, so this stays `V2` and the exact required User-Agent format stays unestablished. It is consistent with `PSR-SEC-008` and `PSR-SEC-009`. **The plan may not treat the exact header format as known.** Note also that the required contact string is a real-world identity the owner must choose; selecting it is a Phase-3B decision and is not made here. |
| `PSR-SEC-047` | SEC (via search-index summaries) | `SECONDARY` | **`V2`** | The SEC states "Please do not use the SEC seal or any of the other logos or artwork from this site", and that "SEC", the EDGAR logo, and the names EDGAR, EDGARLink and EDGARLink Online are the SEC's registered trademarks which may not be used in a trade name, trademark or domain name of an SEC- or EDGAR-related business without a licence. | [SEC.gov \| Privacy Information / website policies](https://www.sec.gov/about/privacy-information) | 2026-08-27 | Corroborates the trademark carve-out in `PSR-SEC-002` at unchanged secondary strength. It is a **branding** restriction, not a content restriction, and does not bear on ingesting or retaining filing content. With sec.gov blocked and the archive blocked, **no route to verbatim SEC policy text exists from this environment**, and every EDGAR field-level claim stays `V2` until it is verified from an environment that can reach sec.gov — which is Phase-3B work, not a planning input. |

A targeted search surfaced **Nasdaq exchange market-data** agreements instead — the Nasdaq OMX Global Data Subscriber Agreement and the US Equities and Options Data Policies. | [Terms \| Nasdaq Data Link](https://data.nasdaq.com/terms) | 2026-08-27 | **Recorded chiefly as a trap to avoid.** The documents that *are* retrievable govern exchange market-data entitlements — a different product family from Nasdaq Data Link's publisher catalogue, with a different non-professional test and different redistribution rules. Independently useful as a description of what a *complete* answer looks like — two availability axes, filing date and product delivery date, which is exactly the public/provider distinction the KalpaMani contract draws. **No such product is available at a published personal-use price.** |

## Retrieval failures, recorded rather than papered over

Sources that could not be read in this research round are `U` rows above. The recurring
causes, because they shape what the plan may claim:

| Cause | Effect on the plan |
|---|---|
| `data.sec.gov` and `www.sec.gov` document pages returned HTTP 403 | every EDGAR field-level claim stays `U`; verifying it is Phase-3A work, not a settled input |
| Vendor pricing rendered client-side by JavaScript | tier prices unobtainable from the static page; recorded as `U` with the attempts listed |
| Pricing behind login or an interactive calculator | recorded as `U` or `Q`; never estimated |
| Vendor documents historical depth nowhere | the decisive question for a borrow provider is unanswered, so that provider stays a candidate |

## Maintenance rule

Prices, licence terms and coverage change. Blueprint V2.1's appendix already requires:
*"Re-verify official documentation immediately before implementation and before enabling
live automation."*

**Every row in this register must be re-verified before the purchase it supports**
(authorization A3), and the accessed date updated. A row whose source no longer says what it
is recorded as saying is a defect in this document, not a rounding error.
